Lemma 6.7

The operators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_b=b(a-\alpha )+T_b+S$$\end{document}Ab=b(a-α)+Tb+S satisfy the condition (1) in Proposition 6.2.

Proof

By applying Lemma 6.4 and that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S=K-C\in \mathcal {L}$$\end{document}S=K-C∈L, Proposition 6.1 implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_b$$\end{document}Ab generates a strongly continuous semigroup. Similarly to the proof of Lemma 6.4, but now applying that S is skewadjoint (recall (6.9)), the following energy estimate on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta =e^{\tau A_b}\Theta _0$$\end{document}Θ=eτAbΘ0 shows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_b=b(a-\alpha )+T_b+S$$\end{document}Ab=b(a-α)+Tb+S is a contraction\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{2}\partial _\tau \int _{\mathbb {R}^2}|\Theta |^2\,\textrm{d}X&=\int _{\mathbb {R}^2}\Theta A_b\Theta \,\textrm{d}X =\int _{\mathbb {R}^2}\Theta (A_b-S)\Theta \,\textrm{d}X \\&= b(a-\alpha -1)\int _{\mathbb {R}^2}|\Theta |^2\,\textrm{d}X\le 0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}12∂τ∫R2|Θ|2dX=∫R2ΘAbΘdX=∫R2Θ(Ab-S)ΘdX=b(a-α-1)∫R2|Θ|2dX≤0.In the last inequality we use our choice of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<a<\varepsilon <1+\alpha $$\end{document}0<a<ε<1+α (recall (2.8)). The continuity in b follows analogously to that of the operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$T_b$$\end{document}Tb in the proof of Lemma 6.1. □ □

Since condition (1) has been verified in Lemma 6.7, condition (2) in Lemma 6.6, and condition (3) in Theorem 3.3, Proposition 6.2 implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\Theta }$$\end{document}Θ¯ is self-similarly unstable.

The Eigenfunction

In this section we prove that the eigenfunction\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ W_b\in \textrm{Ker}(L_b-\lambda _b) $$\end{document}Wb∈Ker(Lb-λb)associated with the eigenvalue \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _b$$\end{document}λb appearing in Proposition 6.2 is smooth and compactly supported.

Given m≥ 5m≥5, we fix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b=b_m$$\end{document}b=bm satisfying6.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0<b <\frac{\Re \lambda _b}{m+3}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0<b<ℜλbm+3.Thus, from now on, we will denote W and λ λ instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_b$$\end{document}Wb and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\lambda _b$$\end{document}λb to alleviate the notation.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$U_{jn}$$\end{document}Ujn is invariant under \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_b$$\end{document}Lb and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ W=\sum _{j\in \mathbb {Z}} W_{jn}(R)e^{ijn \theta }, $$\end{document}W=∑j∈ZWjn(R)eijnθ,each (non-zero) projection \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}(R)e^{ijn \theta }\in U_{jn}$$\end{document}Wjn(R)eijnθ∈Ujn is also an eigenfunction. For j=0j=0, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L_b W_{0}=\lambda _b W_{0}$$\end{document}LbW0=λbW0 reads as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ b(a+R\partial _R)W_{0}=\lambda _b W_{0}, $$\end{document}b(a+R∂R)W0=λbW0,We necessarily have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{0}=0$$\end{document}W0=0. Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ 0\ne W_{jn}(r)e^{ijn\theta }\in \textrm{Ker}(L_b-\lambda )\cap U_{jn} $$\end{document}0≠Wjn(r)eijnθ∈Ker(Lb-λ)∩Ujn for some j≠ 0j≠0. Moreover, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{Ker}(L_b-\lambda )$$\end{document}Ker(Lb-λ) is finite dimensional (see Lemma A.2), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}$$\end{document}Wjn is null for all but a finite number of (non-zero) j’s.

Proposition 6.4

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W\in \textrm{Ker}(L_b-\lambda )\cap U_{jn}$$\end{document}W∈Ker(Lb-λ)∩Ujn with j≠ 0j≠0. Then,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ W\in C^\infty (\mathbb {R}^2\setminus \{0\}) \cap C_c^\gamma (\mathbb {R}^2), $$\end{document}W∈C∞(R2\{0})∩Ccγ(R2),with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\gamma =(\frac{\Re \lambda }{b}-(a-\alpha ))\ge m+2$$\end{document}γ=(ℜλb-(a-α))≥m+2. Moreover,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ W_{jn}(R) =C_0 R^{\frac{\lambda -b(a-\alpha )+ijn C}{b}}e^{-\frac{ijn}{b}U(R)}, $$\end{document}Wjn(R)=C0Rλ-b(a-α)+ijnCbe-ijnbU(R),for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R\le \frac{r_1}{2}$$\end{document}R≤r12, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_0$$\end{document}C0 is a constant, and C and U(R) are given in Proposition 5.3.

Proof

The self-similar stability equation (2.23) can be rewritten as6.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} & \left( (\lambda -b(a-\alpha ))+ijn\frac{\bar{V}_\phi }{R}-bR\partial _R\right) W_{jn}\nonumber \\ & \quad +in\, C_\alpha \partial _R\bar{\Theta }\int _0^\infty I_{jn,\alpha }(S) W_{jn}\left( \frac{R}{S}\right) \frac{\,\textrm{d}S}{S^{1+\alpha }} =0. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}(λ-b(a-α))+ijnV¯ϕR-bR∂RWjn+inCα∂RΘ¯∫0∞Ijn,α(S)WjnRSdSS1+α=0.We remark that (6.11) is well defined since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W\in D(L_b)$$\end{document}W∈D(Lb) by construction. On the interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,r_1-\varepsilon )$$\end{document}(0,r1-ε), we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _R\bar{\Theta }=0$$\end{document}∂RΘ¯=0. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}$$\end{document}Wjn solves an ODE that can be solved explicitly by applying the formula for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{V}_\phi $$\end{document}V¯ϕ from Proposition 5.3. We obtain that, on the interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,r_1-\varepsilon )$$\end{document}(0,r1-ε), it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ W_{jn}(R) =C_0 R^{\frac{(\lambda -b(a-\alpha ))+ijn C}{b}}e^{-\frac{ijn}{b}U(R)} $$\end{document}Wjn(R)=C0R(λ-b(a-α))+ijnCbe-ijnbU(R)for some new constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_0$$\end{document}C0. On the interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(r_2+\varepsilon ,\infty )$$\end{document}(r2+ε,∞), since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _R\bar{\Theta }=0$$\end{document}∂RΘ¯=0, we deduce that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ |W_{jn}(R)| =C_1 R^{\frac{\Re \lambda -b(a-\alpha )}{b}}. $$\end{document}|Wjn(R)|=C1Rℜλ-b(a-α)b.In this case, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}\in L^2$$\end{document}Wjn∈L2 and by (6.10), necessarily \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_1=0$$\end{document}C1=0. As a consequence, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textrm{supp}(W_{jn})\subset [0,r_2+\varepsilon ]$$\end{document}supp(Wjn)⊂[0,r2+ε]. We have checked that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}$$\end{document}Wjn is smooth outside the interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B_\varepsilon ([r_1,r_2])$$\end{document}Bε([r1,r2]). Let us check that it is smooth inside the interval \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I_\varepsilon =B_{2\varepsilon }([r_1,r_2])$$\end{document}Iε=B2ε([r1,r2]). Notice that the last integrand in (6.11) is supported on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S\ge \frac{R}{r_2+\varepsilon }$$\end{document}S≥Rr2+ε. By bootstrapping, the same formula allows us to prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$W_{jn}\in H^k(I_\varepsilon )$$\end{document}Wjn∈Hk(Iε) for any k≥ 1k≥1. □ □

Non-Linear Instability

In this section we prove Theorem 3.5. We emphasize that the proof works for b>0b>0, which establishes the Sobolev non-uniqueness result (Theorem 3.1), as well as for b=0b=0, which demonstrates the existence of unstable vortices exhibiting non-uniqueness at t=-∞ t=-∞ (Theorem 1.2). In this section, m≥ 5m≥5 is fixed (except for the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym and the results related to its properties) and a corresponding b is also fixed in the regime7.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0\le b <\frac{\Re \lambda }{m+3}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0≤b<ℜλm+3.In order to prove Theorem 3.5, we construct a sequence of approximate solutions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}Θk(q) to (2.24) that satisfy the decay condition (2.26) uniformly in both k and q, and then we pass to the limit in a suitable Sobolev space. Indeed, we consider the weighted Hilbert space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H_\omega ^m$$\end{document}Hωm given by the norm\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert f\Vert _{H_\omega ^m} :=\Vert f\omega \Vert _{L^2} +\sum _{0<|K|\le m}\Vert \partial _X^K f\omega \Vert _{L^2}, $$\end{document}‖f‖Hωm:=‖fω‖L2+∑0<|K|≤m‖∂XKfω‖L2,where ω ω is the standard radial weight\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \omega (X) =\langle X\rangle ^2 =1+R^2. $$\end{document}ω(X)=⟨X⟩2=1+R2.Firstly, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}k∈N, we consider the unique solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{\text {cor}}$$\end{document}Θkcor to (2.24)7.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} (\partial _\tau - Remark 7.1

On the one hand, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\Theta } \in C_c^\infty $$\end{document}Θ¯∈Cc∞ (Theorem 3.3) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta ^{\text {lin}} \in C_c^{m+2}$$\end{document}Θlin∈Ccm+2 (Proposition 5.4 for b=0b=0, and Proposition 6.4 for b>0b>0), the initial datum satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\Theta } + \epsilon \Theta ^{\text {lin}}(-k) \in C_c^{m+2}$$\end{document}Θ¯+ϵΘlin(-k)∈Ccm+2. On the other hand, for b>0b>0, the force (7.5) remains smooth and compactly supported for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \ge t_k$$\end{document}t≥tk. Recall that f=0f=0 for b=0b=0. Moreover, by construction, both the initial datum and the force have finite Hamiltonian. Therefore, for all k, q, the solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}Θk(q) to this linearized system belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C([-k,T], H^{m+2} \cap \dot{H}^{\frac{\alpha -2}{2}})$$\end{document}C([-k,T],Hm+2∩H˙α-22) for all T > -kT>-k.

As we explained in the intro, the use of the Duhamel formula allows gaining extra exponential decay, but at the cost of having to control the bound under a stronger norm. In Sect. 7.2 we introduce the energy space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m\hookrightarrow H_\omega ^m\cap L_n^2$$\end{document}Ym↪Hωm∩Ln2. Since we need to work in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym (instead of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H_\omega ^m$$\end{document}Hωm) we must ensure that our approximate solution remains in this space globally in time.

Proposition 7.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k,q\in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}k,q∈N. For every T>-kT>-k, the unique solution \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}Θk(q) to (7.6), (7.7) satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Theta _k^{(q)}\in L^\infty ([-k,T];Y^{m+1})\cap C([-k,T];Y^{m}). $$\end{document}Θk(q)∈L∞([-k,T];Ym+1)∩C([-k,T];Ym).Moreover, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}Θk(q) remains compactly supported.

Proof

We will prove it in Sect. 7.3 after introducing the space \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym. □ □

Due to Proposition 7.1 and recalling (7.7), given some fixed \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _0$$\end{document}δ0 in the regime\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0<\delta _0<c_{(m,0)}:=2^{-\frac{m(m+3)}{2}}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0<δ0<c(m,0):=2-m(m+3)2,we can define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k<\tau _k^{(q)}\le 0$$\end{document}-k<τk(q)≤0 to be the largest non-positive time such that7.8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Theta ^{(q)}_k(\tau )\Vert _{Y^m}\le e^{(1+\delta _0)\Re \lambda \tau } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Θk(q)(τ)‖Ym≤e(1+δ0)ℜλτfor any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \tau _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤τk(q). The purpose of the notation \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_{(m,0)}$$\end{document}c(m,0) will become clear later in the text. Our task is to show that the times \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}τk(q) satisfying (7.8) do not diverge to -∞ -∞ as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k,q\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}k,q→∞. For this purpose, we will use a bootstrapping argument to improve the bound (7.8). This is the content of the following lemma, whose proof will take most of this section.

Lemma 7.1

There exists C>0C>0, independent of k and q, such that7.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Theta ^{(q)}_k(\tau )\Vert _{Y^m}\le C e^{(1+c_{(m,0)})\Re \lambda \tau } \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Θk(q)(τ)‖Ym≤Ce(1+c(m,0))ℜλτfor all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \min _{p\le q}\tau _k^{(p)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤minp≤qτk(p).

Proof

We will prove it in Sects. 7.4-7.8. □ □

As a corollary of this lemma, we obtain the required lower bound for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}τk(q).

Corollary 7.1

It holds that7.10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \bar{\tau } :=\inf _{k,q}\tau _k^{(q)} > -\infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τ¯:=infk,qτk(q)>-∞.

Proof

We prove it by contradiction. Suppose that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\tau }=-\infty $$\end{document}τ¯=-∞. Then, there must exist a sequence satisfying7.11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} 0>\tau _{k_j}^{(q_j)}\rightarrow -\infty , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}0>τkj(qj)→-∞,as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$j\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}j→∞. We can assume, without loss of generality, that7.12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tau _{k_j}^{(q_j)}=\min _{p\le q_j}\tau _{k_j}^{(p)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}τkj(qj)=minp≤qjτkj(p).Otherwise, it would suffice to replace \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_j$$\end{document}qj by the p that minimizes (7.12). By definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau _k^{(q)}$$\end{document}τk(q) in (7.8), we have7.13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\| \Theta ^{(q_j)}_{k_j}\left( \tau _{k_j}^{(q_j)}\right) \right\| _{Y^m}= e^{(1+\delta _0)\Re \lambda \tau _{k_j}^{(q_j)}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Θj(qj)τkj(qj)Ym=e(1+δ0)ℜλτkj(qj).Hence, by combining (7.9) with (7.13), we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ C e^{(c_{(m,0)}-\delta _0)\Re \lambda \tau _{k_j}^{(q_j)}}\ge 1, $$\end{document}Ce(c(m,0)-δ0)ℜλτkj(qj)≥1,which contradicts (7.11), due to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _0<c_{(m,0)}$$\end{document}δ0<c(m,0). □ □

In the next section, we show how Theorem 3.5 is proven once we know that (7.10) holds. Therefore, in order to conclude it will remain to check Lemma 7.1.

Proof of Theorem 3.5

Firstly, we take the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}q→∞, to recover the solutions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{\text {cor}}$$\end{document}Θkcor. Secondly, we take the limit \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k\rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}k→∞. Notice that the Hamiltonian remains finite since the sequence of classical solutions \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta _k^{\text {cor}}$$\end{document}Θkcor satisfy the Hamiltonian identity, which provides a uniform bound in k due to Remark 7.1.

Weighted Energy Space

As we explained in Sect. 3, it will be necessary to switch between Cartesian and polar coordinates. The following lemmas relate the partial derivatives of any order in both coordinate systems. We present a proof in Appendix A.2 for completeness.

Lemma 7.2

(From Cartesian to Polar derivatives) For any multi-index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$K=(k_1,k_2)$$\end{document}K=(k1,k2) with |K|>0|K|>0, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \partial _X^Kf =\sum _{0<|J|\le |K|}p_J^K\frac{\partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f}{R^{|K|-j_1}}, $$\end{document}∂XKf=∑0<|J|≤|K|pJK∂R1∂ϕ2fR|K|-j1,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p_J^K=p_J^K(\cos \phi ,\sin \phi )$$\end{document}pJK=pJK(cosϕ,sinϕ) is a |K|-homogeneous polynomial.

Lemma 7.3

(From Polar to Cartesian derivatives) For any multi-index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$J=(j_1,j_2)$$\end{document}J=(j1,j2) with |J|>0|J|>0, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f =\sum _{\begin{array}{c} 0<|K|\le |J| \\ j_1\le |K| \end{array}} q_K^J R^{|K|-j_1}\partial _X^K f, $$\end{document}∂R1∂ϕ2f=∑0<|K|≤|J|j1≤|K|qKJR|K|-j1∂XKf,where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$q_K^J=q_K^J(\cos \phi ,\sin \phi )$$\end{document}qKJ=qKJ(cosϕ,sinϕ) is a |K|-homogeneous polynomial.

In Lemmas 7.4 and 7.5 we will see that indeed\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ Y^m=Z^m\hookrightarrow H^m_\omega , $$\end{document}Ym=Zm↪Hωm,as well as other embeddings that will be crucial during the energy estimates. Embeddings

Note that the inequality7.14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f\Vert _{H^m}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f‖Hm≲‖f‖Zmfollows by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z^m$$\end{document}Zm. Lemma 7.4

For any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le |K|\le m$$\end{document}0≤|K|≤m and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in Z^m$$\end{document}f∈Zm,7.15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial _X^K f \, \omega \Vert _{H^{m-|K|}}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂XKfω‖Hm-|K|≲‖f‖Zm.In particular,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f\Vert _{H^m_\omega }\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f‖Hωm≲‖f‖Zm.

Proof

Note that the case |K|=m|K|=m follows directly by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Z^m$$\end{document}Zm. We start by proving the case |K|=0|K|=0. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f \omega \Vert _{L^2}\le \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}$$\end{document}‖fω‖L2≤‖f‖Zm, it is enough to see that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \partial ^L_X (f\omega )\Vert _{L^2}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}$$\end{document}‖∂XL(fω)‖L2≲‖f‖Zm for every |L|=m|L|=m. Therefore, we let |L|=m|L|=m and apply the Leibniz rule\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial ^L_X (f\omega )=\sum _{(0,0)\le J\le L}{L \atopwithdelims ()J}\partial ^{L-J}_X f \, \partial ^J_X\omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂XL(fω)=∑(0,0)≤J≤LLJ∂XL-Jf∂XJω.Note that, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\omega (X)=1+X_1^2+X_2^2$$\end{document}ω(X)=1+X12+X22, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial ^J_X\omega =0$$\end{document}∂XJω=0 for J≠ (0,0),(1,0),(0,1),(2,0),(0,2)J≠(0,0),(1,0),(0,1),(2,0),(0,2). We deal with these cases separately. If J=(0,0)J=(0,0), then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial ^{L-J}_X f \, \partial ^J_X\omega \Vert _{L^2}=\Vert \partial ^{L}_X f \, \omega \Vert _{L^2}\le \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂XL-Jf∂XJω‖L2=‖∂XLfω‖L2≤‖f‖Zm.If J=(1,0)J=(1,0) or J=(0,1)J=(0,1) we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial ^J_X\omega =2X_1$$\end{document}∂XJω=2X1 or \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2X_2$$\end{document}2X2, respectively. Therefore, since |L-J|=m-1|L-J|=m-1,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial ^{L-J}_X f \, \partial ^J_X\omega \Vert _{L^2}=\left\| \frac{\partial ^{L-J}_X f}{R} \frac{R\partial ^J_X\omega }{\omega } \omega \right\| _{L^2}\le 2\left\| \frac{\partial ^{L-J}_X f}{R^{m-|L-J|}} \omega \right\| _{L^2}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂XL-Jf∂XJω‖L2=∂XL-JfRR∂XJωωωL2≤2∂XL-JfRm-|L-J|ωL2≲‖f‖Zm.Lastly, if J=(2,0)J=(2,0) or J=(0,2)J=(0,2) we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _X^J\omega =2$$\end{document}∂XJω=2, thus, by (7.14),\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial ^{L-J}_X f \, \partial ^J_X\omega \Vert _{L^2}=2\Vert \partial ^{L-J}_X f\Vert _{L^2} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂XL-Jf∂XJω‖L2=2‖∂XL-Jf‖L2≲‖f‖Zm.We now proceed inductively on |K|. Let 1<|K|≤ m1<|K|≤m and suppose that (7.15) holds for every K'K′ with |K'|=|K|-1|K′|=|K|-1. Take |J|=1|J|=1 with J≤ KJ≤K, then\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \partial ^K_Xf \omega \Vert _{H^{m-|K|}}&=\Vert \partial ^{J}_X(\partial ^{K-J}_X f\omega )-\partial ^{K-J}_Xf\partial _X^J\omega \Vert _{H^{m-|K|}}\\&=\left\| \partial ^{J}_X(\partial ^{K-J}_X f\omega )-\partial ^{K-J}_Xf\frac{\partial _X^J\omega }{\omega }\omega \right\| _{H^{m-|K|}}\\&\lesssim \Vert \partial ^{K-J}_X f\omega \Vert _{H^{m-(|K|-1)}}+\Vert \partial ^{K-J}_X f \omega \Vert _{H^{m-|K|}}\\&\lesssim \Vert \partial _X^{K-J}f\omega \Vert _{H^{m-|K-J|}} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖∂XKfω‖Hm-|K|=‖∂XJ(∂XK-Jfω)-∂XK-Jf∂XJω‖Hm-|K|=∂XJ(∂XK-Jfω)-∂XK-Jf∂XJωωωHm-|K|≲‖∂XK-Jfω‖Hm-(|K|-1)+‖∂XK-Jfω‖Hm-|K|≲‖∂XK-Jfω‖Hm-|K-J|≲‖f‖Zm,where K-JK-J plays the role of K'K′. □ □

Lemma 7.5

It holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m} \simeq \Vert f\Vert _{Y^m}. $$\end{document}‖f‖Zm≃‖f‖Ym.Moreover,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert f\Vert _{Y^m}&\simeq \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Y}^m}:=\Vert f\omega \Vert _{L^2} +\sum _{0<|J|\le r+j_2\le m}\left\| \frac{\partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f}{R^{m-r}}\omega \right\| _{L^2},\\ \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}&\simeq \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Z}^m}:=\Vert f\omega \Vert _{L^2} +\sum _{0<|K|\le r\le m}\left\| \frac{\partial _X^K f}{R^{m-r}}\omega \right\| _{L^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖f‖Ym≃‖f‖Y¯m:=‖fω‖L2+∑0<|J|≤r+j2≤m∂R1∂ϕ2fRm-rωL2,‖f‖Zm≃‖f‖Z¯m:=‖fω‖L2+∑0<|K|≤r≤m∂XKfRm-rωL2.

Proof

To prove the equivalences of norms we prove the following sequence of inequalities:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f\Vert _{Z^m} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Y^m} \le \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Y}^m} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Z}^m} \lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}.$$\end{document}‖f‖Zm≲‖f‖Ym≤‖f‖Y¯m≲‖f‖Z¯m≲‖f‖Zm.To prove \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Y^m}$$\end{document}‖f‖Zm≲‖f‖Ym we use Lemma 7.2 to write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{\partial _X^K f}{R^{m-|K|}}\omega =\sum _{0<|J|\le |K|}p_J^K\frac{\partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f}{R^{m-j_1}}\omega $$\end{document}∂XKfRm-|K|ω=∑0<|J|≤|K|pJK∂R1∂ϕ2fRm-j1ωfor any 0<|K|≤ m0<|K|≤m and then apply the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym.

The inequality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f\Vert _{Y^m}\le \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Y}^m}$$\end{document}‖f‖Ym≤‖f‖Y¯m is trivial. For the inequality \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Y}^m}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Z}^m}$$\end{document}‖f‖Y¯m≲‖f‖Z¯m let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<|J|\le r+j_2\le m$$\end{document}0<|J|≤r+j2≤m and use Lemma 7.3 to write\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{\partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f}{R^{m-r}}\omega =\sum _{\begin{array}{c} 0<|K|\le |J| \\ j_1\le |K| \end{array}} q_K^J \frac{\partial _X^K f}{R^{m-(r-j_1+|K|)}}\omega .$$\end{document}∂R1∂ϕ2fRm-rω=∑0<|K|≤|J|j1≤|K|qKJ∂XKfRm-(r-j1+|K|)ω.Then the inequality follows by using the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{Z}^m$$\end{document}Z¯m since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|K|\le r-j_1+|K|\le m$$\end{document}|K|≤r-j1+|K|≤m.

Finally, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert f\Vert _{\bar{Z}^m}\lesssim \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}$$\end{document}‖f‖Z¯m≲‖f‖Zm follows by decoupling\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{\left| \partial _X^K f\right| }{R^{m-r}}\omega \le \frac{\left| \partial _X^K f\right| }{R^{m-|K|}}\omega 1_B +\left| \partial _X^K f\right| \omega 1_{B^c}\le \Vert f\Vert _{Z^m}+\Vert f\Vert _{H^m_\omega }, $$\end{document}∂XKfRm-rω≤∂XKfRm-|K|ω1B+∂XKfω1Bc≤‖f‖Zm+‖f‖Hωm,for any \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<|K|\le r\le m$$\end{document}0<|K|≤r≤m, and then applying Lemma 7.4. □ □

The next lemma relates \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H_\omega ^m$$\end{document}Hωm with classical Sobolev spaces. The embedding is not optimal, but since m is arbitrarily large, we present a simple version that is enough for our purposes.

Lemma 7.6

For all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1\le p\le \infty $$\end{document}1≤p≤∞,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ H_\omega ^m\hookrightarrow W^{m-2,p}. $$\end{document}Hωm↪Wm-2,p.

Proof

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in H_\omega ^m$$\end{document}f∈Hωm. In the next lemma, we show that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^{m+1}(\Omega )$$\end{document}Ym+1(Ω) is compact in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} bounded subsets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}Ω⊂R2 and arbitrary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m(\Omega )$$\end{document}Ym(Ω) is given as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} Lemma 7.7

The embedding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^{m+1}(\Omega )\hookrightarrow Y^m(\Omega )$$\end{document}Ym+1(Ω)↪Ym(Ω) is compact for any bounded subset \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}Ω⊂R2.

Proof

Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f\in Y^{m+1}(\Omega )$$\end{document}f∈Ym+1(Ω) and 0<|J|≤ m0<|J|≤m, we denote\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ f_J=\frac{\partial ^J f}{R^{m-j_1}}=\frac{\partial _R^{j_1}\partial _\phi ^{j_2}f}{R^{m-j_1}}. $$\end{document}fJ=∂JfRm-j1=∂R1∂ϕ2fRm-j1.On the one hand,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \partial _R f_J =\frac{\partial ^{J+(1,0)} f}{R^{(m+1)-(j_1+1)}} -(m-j_1)\frac{\partial ^J f}{R^{m+1-j_1}}. $$\end{document}∂RfJ=∂J+(1,0)fR(m+1)-(j1+1)-(m-j1)∂JfRm+1-j1.On the other hand,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{\partial _\phi f_J}{R} =\frac{\partial ^{J+(0,1)} f}{R^{(m+1)-j_1}}. $$\end{document}∂ϕfJR=∂J+(0,1)fR(m+1)-j1.Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_J\in H^1(\Omega )$$\end{document}fJ∈H1(Ω). The statement follows by applying that the embedding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H^1(\Omega )\hookrightarrow L^2(\Omega )$$\end{document}H1(Ω)↪L2(Ω) is compact. The weight ω ω does not play any role here. □ □

Next, we check that the vortex and eigenfunction belong to these weighted energy spaces.

Lemma 7.8

It holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \bar{\Theta }\in \bigcap _{m\in \mathbb {N}}Y^m. $$\end{document}Θ¯∈⋂m∈NYm.

Proof

By construction, we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\Theta }\in C_c^\infty (\mathbb {R}^2)$$\end{document}Θ¯∈Cc∞(R2) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\bar{\Theta }(X)$$\end{document}Θ¯(X) constant on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|X|\le \frac{1}{4}$$\end{document}|X|≤14. □ □

Lemma 7.9

There exists C>0C>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert \Theta ^{\text {lin}}(\tau )\Vert _{Y^{m+2}} \le C e^{\Re \lambda \tau }, $$\end{document}‖Θlin(τ)‖Ym+2≤Ceℜλτ,for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tau \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}τ∈R.

Proof

Recall that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta ^{\text {lin}}(\tau ,X)=\Re (e^{\lambda _b\tau }W_b(X))$$\end{document}Θlin(τ,X)=ℜ(eλbτWb(X)) (see Sect. 6.7). The statement follows by Proposition 5.4 for b=0b=0, and by Proposition 6.4 for b>0b>0. □ □

We conclude this section by controlling the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty $$\end{document}L∞ norm of the velocity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V=-\nabla ^\perp \Lambda ^{\alpha -2}\Theta $$\end{document}V=-∇⊥Λα-2Θ, namely7.16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert V\Vert _{L^\infty }\lesssim \Vert \Theta \Vert _{H_\omega ^m}\lesssim \Vert \Theta \Vert _{Y^m}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖V‖L∞≲‖Θ‖Hωm≲‖Θ‖Ym.This follows from the following lemma in combination with Lemmas 7.4- 7.6:

Lemma 7.10

For α =0α=0 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2<p\le \infty $$\end{document}2<p≤∞, there exists C>0C>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert V\Vert _{L^\infty } \le C(\Vert \Theta \Vert _{L^1}+\Vert \Theta \Vert _{L^p}). $$\end{document}‖V‖L∞≤C(‖Θ‖L1+‖Θ‖Lp).For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0<\alpha \le 1$$\end{document}0<α≤1 and ε >0ϵ>0, there exists C>0C>0 such that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert V\Vert _{L^\infty }\le C\Vert \Theta \Vert _{H^{1+\epsilon }}. $$\end{document}‖V‖L∞≤C‖Θ‖H1+ϵ.

Proof

For α =1α=1, simply \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$V\in H^{1+\epsilon }\hookrightarrow L^\infty $$\end{document}V∈H1+ϵ↪L∞ by the Morrey inequality. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0\le \alpha <1$$\end{document}0≤α<1, we bound\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |V(X)|\le C_\alpha \int _{\mathbb {R}^2}\frac{|\Theta (Y)|}{|X-Y|^{1+\alpha }}\,\textrm{d}Y. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|V(X)|≤Cα∫R2|Θ(Y)||X-Y|1+αdY.We split \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathbb {R}^2$$\end{document}R2 into |X-Y|>1|X-Y|>1 and |X-Y|<1|X-Y|<1. Let α =0α=0. Baseline \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 estimate

In this section, we improve the exponential decay of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Vert \Theta ^{(q)}_k\Vert _{L^2}$$\end{document}‖Θk(q)‖L2. In the next sections, we will use this baseline estimate to address the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym-norm for the proof of Lemma 7.1.

Lemma 7.11

There exists C>0C>0 such that7.20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Vert \Theta ^{(q)}_k(\tau )\Vert _{L^2}\le C e^{2\Re \lambda \tau }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}‖Θk(q)(τ)‖L2≤Ce2ℜλτ,for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \min _{p\le q}\tau _k^{(p)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤minp≤qτk(p).

Proof

The solution to (7.6) satisfies the Duhamel formula\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Inductive Energy Estimates

In this section we deal with the weighted norms appearing in the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym-norm for the proof of Lemma 7.1. We start by rewriting (7.6) as7.22\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \partial _\tau \Theta -b(a-\alpha )\Theta +(\bar{V}-bX+\tilde{V})\cdot \nabla \Theta +V\cdot \nabla \bar{\Theta }+\tilde{V}\cdot \nabla \Theta ^{\text {lin}}=0, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂τΘ-b(a-α)Θ+(V¯-bX+V~)·∇Θ+V·∇Θ¯+V~·∇Θlin=0,where we have abbreviated7.23\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \Theta =\Theta _k^{(q)}, \quad \quad V=V_k^{(q)}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Θ=Θk(q),V=Vk(q),and also7.24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{\Theta }=\epsilon \Theta ^{\text {lin}}+\epsilon ^2 \Theta _k^{(q-1)}, \quad \quad \tilde{V}=\epsilon V^{\text {lin}}+\epsilon ^2 V_k^{(q-1)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Θ~=ϵΘlin+ϵ2Θk(q-1),V~=ϵVlin+ϵ2Vk(q-1).Notice that Θ Θ and V correspond to step q, while \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{\Theta }$$\end{document}Θ~ and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{V}$$\end{document}V~ refer to step q-1q-1. We start by proving that it is possible to improve the bound of the weighted \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 norm in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym by using the baseline estimate in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2.

In the next proposition, we prove an energy estimate that holds for all multi-indices J.

Proposition 7.2

(Energy estimate) For any multi-index J with |J|>0|J|>0, it holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} Proposition 7.3

(Inductive energy estimate) Let J be a multi-index with 0<|J|≤ m0<|J|≤m. Assume that for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,0)\ne I\prec J$$\end{document}(0,0)≠I≺J there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C_I>0$$\end{document}CI>0 such that7.29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left\| \frac{\partial ^I\Theta (\tau )}{R^{m-i_1}}\omega \right\| _{L^2} \le C_I e^{(1+c_I)\Re \lambda \tau }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂IΘ(τ)Rm-i1ωL2≤CIe(1+cI)ℜλτ,for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \min _{p\le q}\tau _k^{(p)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤minp≤qτk(p). Then, there exists \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_J>0$$\end{document}DJ>0 such that7.30\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \left| \int \mathcal {H}_{J}\frac{\partial ^J\Theta }{R^{m-j_1}}\omega \,\textrm{d}X\right| (\tau ) \le D_J e^{2(1+c_J)\Re \lambda \tau }, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫HJ∂JΘRm-j1ωdX(τ)≤DJe2(1+cJ)ℜλτ,for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \min _{p\le q}\tau _k^{(p)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤minp≤qτk(p). As a consequence, the bound (7.29) is also satisfied for J. In particular, no assumption is needed to obtain (7.29) for J=(0,1)J=(0,1).

Proof

We prove the bound (7.30) for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{\text {rad}}$$\end{document}Hrad, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{\text {com}}$$\end{document}Hcom and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{\text {quad}}$$\end{document}Hquad in Sections 7.7, 7.8 and 7.9, respectively. Assuming that we have already proven (7.30), we show that (7.29) is also satisfied for J. By applying Proposition 7.2 and the bound (7.30), together with (7.1), we get7.31\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \left\| \frac{\partial ^J\Theta }{R^{m-j_1}}\omega \right\| _{L^2}^2&\le D_J\int _{-k}^{\tau } e^{2b(m+2)(\tau -\tau ')+2(1+c_J)\Re \lambda \tau '}\,\textrm{d}\tau '\\&\le \frac{1}{2}\frac{D_J}{(1+c_J)\Re \lambda -b(m+2)} e^{2(1+c_J)\Re \lambda \tau }, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∂JΘRm-j1ωL22≤DJ∫-kτe2b(m+2)(τ-τ′)+2(1+cJ)ℜλτ′dτ′≤12DJ(1+cJ)ℜλ-b(m+2)e2(1+cJ)ℜλτ,for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$-k\le \tau \le \min _{p\le q}\tau _k^{(p)}$$\end{document}-k≤τ≤minp≤qτk(p). □ □

Proof of Lemma 7.1

We apply the inductive energy estimate (Proposition 7.3) on the multi-indices J. Notice that for the first multi-index J=(0,1)J=(0,1) there are no \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(0,0)\ne I\prec J$$\end{document}(0,0)≠I≺J, thus Proposition 7.3 immediately implies (7.29) for J=(0,1)J=(0,1). We now make the induction hypothesis. Given a multi-index J≻ (0,1)J≻(0,1), suppose that (7.29) is satisfied for any multi-index I with I≺ JI≺J. Then, we can apply Proposition 7.3 to deduce that (7.29) is also satisfied for J. Once we have reached the last multi-index J=(m,0)J=(m,0), we have proven that (7.29) is satisfied for any multi-index J with 0<|J|≤ m0<|J|≤m. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$c_J$$\end{document}cJ is decreasing with respect to the ordering ≺ ≺, we deduce (7.9) and thus Lemma 7.1 as well. Recall that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^2$$\end{document}L2 part of the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y^m$$\end{document}Ym norm was estimated in Lemma 7.12.

If |L|=2|L|=2 then |I|=m-1|I|=m-1 and thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\tilde{I}=I$$\end{document}I~=I, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m-(i_1-\tilde{i}_1+|L+K|-1)=0$$\end{document}m-(i1-i~1+|L+K|-1)=0 and therefore for this we have to estimate \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_X^2\Theta \,\omega $$\end{document}DX2Θω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty $$\end{document}L∞. Finally, if |L|=1|L|=1 then we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m-(i_1-\tilde{i}_1+|L+K|-1)$$\end{document}m-(i1-i~1+|L+K|-1) is either 0 or 1, so we have to control \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$D_X\Theta \,\omega $$\end{document}DXΘω and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(D\Theta /R)\,\omega $$\end{document}(DΘ/R)ω in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^\infty $$\end{document}L∞. By applying the Sobolev embedding and Lemmas 7.4-7.5, we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Quadratic Term 2

Lemma 7.16

It holds that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \Vert \mathcal {H}_J^{\text {quad},2}\Vert _{L^2} \lesssim e^{2\Re \lambda \tau }. $$\end{document}‖HJquad,2‖L2≲e2ℜλτ.

Proof

Since the only difference between \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_J^{\text {quad},2}$$\end{document}HJquad,2 and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}_J^{\text {quad},1}$$\end{document}HJquad,1 is the replacement of Θ Θ with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Theta ^{\text{ l }in}$$\end{document}Θlin, the proof follows in an entirely analogous manner. □ □
